Line 313 is in multi_fvar: after induction on Hm, the source wasn't generalized properly, so the IH is `z = y` (motive not depending on source) and Hst : x0 --> y where x0 is not syntactically tm_fvar x. The problem: `intros x v Hm.` then `induction Hm` — Coq inferred motive `fun a b => ?` maybe it didn't generalize x0 to tm_fvar x. Actually in the multi_step case, x0 should have been unified with tm_fvar x because Hm : multi step (tm_fvar x) v. But the error says Hst : x0 --> y, meaning unison didn't substitute x0 := tm_fvar x. Hmm, because induction on Hm generalized the indices t v in the goal, and the motive was fun a b => b = tm_fvar x? Then in the step case, the source x0 remains abstract and there's no equation x0 = tm_fvar x? Inversion-based induction should keep the equation. It seems the induction gave the case for arbitrary x0 with Hst : x0 --> y, and the goal `z = tm_fvar x`; the IH : z = y. The desired contradiction requires x0 = tm_fvar x, which is not available. So the motive didn't capture the dependency.

Let's restructure multi_fvar to avoid this: 
```
Lemma multi_fvar : forall x v, multi step (tm_fvar x) v -> v = tm_fvar x.
Proof.
  intros x v Hm.
  induction Hm as [z | a y z Hst Hm IH].
  - reflexivity.
  - (* Hst : a --> y where a is the source in the step case; but the source here is tm_fvar x? *)
```
The issue: the induction principle for multi: 
```
multi_ind : forall (X) (R) (P : X -> X -> Prop),
  (forall x, P x x) ->
  (forall x y z, R x y -> multi R y z -> P y z -> P x z) ->
  forall x z, multi R x z -> P x z
```
When we do induction Hm with goal `v = tm_fvar x` where Hm : multi step (tm_fvar x) v, the motive would be P a b := b = tm_fvar x (not depending on a), so the step case gives IH : P y z = z = tm_fvar x and premise R x y with x abstract. It doesn't know x = tm_fvar x? Actually, the induction should apply P to the *indices* with the equation from the hypothesis. Coq's induction on an inductive hypothesis with indices will generalize the indices; but here the goal only mentions v (the second index), so the first index (tm_fvar x) is not generalized properly; the resulting case has `a` arbitrary with Hst : a --> y.

The standard fix: generalize the dependent part. Use `remember` or state the lemma with the source as a variable and keep the equality. Better:
```
Lemma multi_fvar : forall x v, multi step (tm_fvar x) v -> v = tm_fvar x.
Proof.
  intros x v Hm.
  remember (tm_fvar x) as t eqn:Et.
  induction Hm as [z | a y z Hst Hm IH].
  - subst. reflexivity.
  - subst a. exfalso. exact (fvar_no_step x y Hst).
```
Hmm, with remember, the induction motive is fun a b => a = tm_fvar x -> b = tm_fvar x? Let's think: after remember, Hm : multi step t v with Et : t = tm_fvar x. Goal v = tm_fvar x. induction Hm generalizes t; motive P a b := (a = tm_fvar x -> b = tm_fvar x)? Actually the goal depends on t only through Et. Hmm.

Simplest: prove multi_fvar by induction on the multi derivation using an explicit generalized statement:
```
Lemma multi_fvar : forall t x v, t = tm_fvar x -> multi step t v -> v = tm_fvar x.
Proof.
  intros t x v Heq Hm. subst t.
  induction Hm as [z | a y z Hst Hm IH].
  - reflexivity.
  - exfalso. inversion Hst. 
```
But again the induction's step case has source `a` abstract... Wait, Hm : multi step (tm_fvar x) v after subst. induction Hm — the first index is `tm_fvar x`, a concrete term, not a variable! In the inductive family multi R x z, if the first index is a concrete application, the induction principle should specialize it: for multi_ind, P is applied to the concrete index and the step case should unify. Hmm, but the error suggests otherwise.

Actually the issue is that when Coq does `induction Hm`, it abstracts the indices that appear in the goal; here the goal is `v = tm_fvar x`, which does not mention the first index. Coq's induction uses the induction principle with P a b := ... and the hypothesis multi step (tm_fvar x) v is an instance. With a concrete index (tm_fvar x), unification in the step case should give a = tm_fvar x. But the error shows Hst : x0 --> y with x0 abstract, meaning Coq generalized the index as a variable. This is because induction generalizes all indices and then applies the principle with equations; when the index is concrete, it uses "dependent elimination" and the equation a = tm_fvar x should be recorded. Hmm.

Let me just avoid induction on multi here: use the multi_step_inv lemma approach, proven by case analysis with `inversion` which keeps equations.

```
Lemma multi_inv : forall t v, multi step t v -> t = v \/ exists y, step t y /\ multi step y v.
Proof.
  intros t v Hm. inversion Hm; subst.
  - left; reflexivity.
  - right. eexists. split; eauto.
Qed.
```
`inversion Hm` on an inductive family with variable indices: yields two cases with the equations properly substituted: multi_refl gives t = v; multi_step gives step t y and multi y v. This is inversion, which uses dependent elimination and should unify t with the source. Then multi_fvar:
```
Lemma multi_fvar : forall x v, multi step (tm_fvar x) v -> v = tm_fvar x.
Proof.
  intros x v Hm.
  destruct (multi_inv _ _ Hm) as [Heq | [y [Hst _]]].
  - symmetry; exact Heq.
  - exfalso. exact (fvar_no_step x y Hst).
Qed.
```
✓ This avoids the problematic induction.

Let me first write R and V and projections, plus R_step, R_fvar/R_true/R_false, and compile.

```coq
(** ** The logical relation *)

Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false)
  | Ty_Arrow A B =>
      forall v, multi step t v -> value v ->
        exists body, v = tm_abs A body /\ lc_at 1 body /\
          (forall u, R A u -> R B (open body u))
  end.

Definition V (T : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow A B =>
      exists body, v = tm_abs A body /\ lc_at 1 body /\
        (forall u, R A u -> R B (open body u))
  end.
```

Potential guard condition issue. Let's compile.

Then:
```coq
Lemma R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> locally_closed t.
Proof. intros T t H. destruct T; simpl in H; exact (proj1 H). Qed.

Lemma R_sn : forall T t, R T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof. intros T t H. destruct T; simpl in H; exact (proj1 (proj2 H)). Qed.

Lemma R_V : forall T t v, R T t -> multi step t v -> value v -> V T v.
Proof.
  intros T t v H Hm Hv. destruct T as [| A B]; simpl in *.
  - exact (proj2 (proj2 H) v Hm Hv).
  - exact (proj2 (proj2 H) v Hm Hv).
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma R_true : R Ty_Bool tm_true.
```

```coq
Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> step t t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  intros T t t' HR Hst. destruct T as [| A B]; simpl in *.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Hval]]. split; [apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption | split].
    + apply SN_step with (t := t); assumption.
    + intros v Hm Hv. apply (Hval v).
      * apply multi_step with (y := t'); assumption.
      * assumption.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Hval]]. split; [apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption | split].
    + apply SN_step with (t := t); assumption.
    + intros v Hm Hv. apply (Hval v).
      * apply multi_step with (y := t'); assumption.
      * assumption.
Qed.
```

Let me write it and test. I'll write the version with inversion patterns.

```coq
Lemma R_app_abs : forall (A B : ty) (body u : tm),
  lc_at 1 body ->
  (forall w, R A w -> R B (open body w)) ->
  R A u ->
  R B (tm_app (tm_abs A body) u).
Proof.
  intros A B body u Hlcbody Hbody Hu.
  assert (Hsn : strongly_normalizing u) by apply (R_sn A u Hu).
  revert A B body Hlcbody Hbody Hu.
  induction Hsn as [u IH].
  intros A B body Hlcbody Hbody Hu.
```

Let me now write R_app. Recall the plan:

```coq
Lemma R_app : forall (A B : ty) (t u : tm),
  R (Ty_Arrow A B) t -> R A u -> R B (tm_app t u).
Proof.
  intros A B t u HRt Hu.
  assert (Hsnt : strongly_normalizing t) by apply (R_sn (Ty_Arrow A B) t HRt).
  revert A B u HRt Hu.
  induction Hsnt as [t Hrec IH].
  intros A B u HRt Hu.
  destruct HRt as [Hlct [Hsnt Hvalt]].
  assert (Hlcapp : locally_closed (tm_app t u)).
  { apply lc_app; [exact Hlct | apply (R_lc A u Hu)]. }
  split; [exact Hlcapp | split].
  - (* SN *)
    apply SN_intro. intros s' Hst.
    inversion Hst as [T t0 v Hlc0 Hv0 | t1 t1' t2 Hst1 Hlct2 | v1 t2 t2' Hv1 Hst2]; subst.
    + (* ST_AppAbs *) ...
    + (* ST_App1 *) ...
    + (* ST_App2 *) ...
  - (* value reducts *)
    intros v Hm Hvval.
    destruct (multi_inv _ _ Hm) as [Heq | [y [Hst Hm']]].
    + subst v. inversion Hvval.
    + inversion Hst as [T t0 v0 Hlc0 Hv0 | t1 t1' t2 Hst1 Hlct2 | v1 t2 t2' Hv1 Hst2]; subst.
      * ...
      * ...
      * ...
Qed.
```

Wait, there's a problem: after `destruct HRt`, we lose the "remembered" Hsnt used for induction. That's fine—the IH is already generalized.

Detailed cases:

SN part:
1. ST_AppAbs: Hst : step (tm_app t u) s' with ST_AppAbs → t = tm_abs T t0, u = v, s' = open t0 v, Hlc0 : locally_closed (tm_abs T t0), Hv0 : value v.
   Need SN (open t0 v). Get V_arrow for t: since t = tm_abs T t0 is a value (v_abs Hlc0), Hvalt t (multi_refl) (value) gives exists body, tm_abs T t0 = tm_abs A body ∧ lc ∧ forall w, R A w -> R B (open body w). Unify T = A, t0 = body. Then apply R_sn B (open body v) with Hbody v Hu (since u = v). ✓
2. ST_App1: t1 = t, t1' = t1', t2 = u, s' = tm_app t1' u, Hst1 : step t t1', Hlct2 : locally_closed u.
   Apply IH t1' Hst1 A B u (R_step (Ty_Arrow A B) t t1' HRt Hst1) Hu. But wait, HRt was destructed! We need the original R (A->B) t. We destructed HRt into components.   revert A B u HRt Hu.

```
Lemma V_reducts_app_aux : forall (A B : ty) (t u : tm),
  R (Ty_Arrow A B) t -> R A u ->
  (forall t', step t t' -> forall A0 B0 u0, R (Ty_Arrow A0 B0) t' -> R A0 u0 -> R B0 (tm_app t' u0)) ->
  forall v, multi step (tm_app t u) v -> value v -> V B v.
Proof.
  intros A B t u HRt Hu IHapp v Hm Hvval.
  destruct (multi_inv _ _ Hm) as [Heq | [y [Hst Hm']]].
  - subst v. inversion Hvval.
  - inversion Hst as [T t0 v0 Hlc0 Hv0 | t1 t1' t2 Hst1 Hlct2 | v1 t2 t2' Hv1 Hst2]; subst.

      * apply (R_app_abs A B body t2' Hlcb Hbody). apply (R_step A u t2' Hu Hst2).
      * exact Hm'.
      * exact Hvval.
Qed.
```

```coq
Definition env_good (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x)).

Lemma env_good_extend : forall Gamma sigma x u T1,
  env_good Gamma sigma ->
  locally_closed u ->
  R T1 u ->
  env_good (update Gamma x T1) (extend x u sigma).
Proof.
  intros Gamma sigma x u T1 [Hlc HR] Hlcu HRu.
  split.
  - intros y. unfold extend.
    destruct (Nat.eqb x y); [exact Hlcu | apply Hlc].
  - intros y U Hy. unfold extend, update in *.
    destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y.
      injection Hy as HU. subst U. exact HRu.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E.
      apply HR. exact Hy.
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, env_good Gamma sigma -> R T (subst_tm sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as [Gamma x T Hx
                 | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 IH
                 | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
                 | Gamma
                 | Gamma].
  - (* T_Var *)
    intros sigma [_ HR]. simpl. apply HR. exact Hx.
  - (* T_Abs *)
    intros sigma [Hlc HR].
    simpl.
    (* body = subst_tm sigma t1; need R (T1->T2) (abs T1 body) *)
    ...
  - (* T_App *)
    intros sigma Hg. simpl.
    apply (R_app T1 T2 (subst_tm sigma t1) (subst_tm sigma t2)).
    + apply IH1. exact Hg.
    + apply IH2. exact Hg.
  - (* T_True *) intros sigma Hg. simpl. apply R_true.
  - (* T_False *) intros sigma Hg. simpl. apply R_false.
Qed.
```

```coq
Definition env_good (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x)).

Lemma env_good_extend : forall Gamma sigma x u T1,
  env_good Gamma sigma ->
  locally_closed u ->
  R T1 u ->
  env_good (update Gamma x T1) (extend x u sigma).
Proof.
  intros Gamma sigma x u T1 [Hlc HR] Hlcu HRu.
  split.
  - intros y. unfold extend.
    destruct (Nat.eqb x y); [exact Hlcu | apply Hlc].
  - intros y U Hy. unfold extend, update in *.
    destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y.
      injection Hy as HU. subst U. exact HRu.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. apply HR. exact Hy.
Qed.
```

Hmm, in the second part: after `unfold extend, update in *`, the goal is `R U (if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y)` and Hy : (if Nat.eqb x y then Some T1 else Gamma y) = Some U. Then destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E. In the true branch, both ifs reduce to u and Some T1. Goal R U u, Hy : Some T1 = Some U. injection Hy as HU gives HU : T1 = U. `subst U` gives goal R T1 u; exact HRu ✓.
In the false branch: goal R U (sigma y), Hy : Gamma y = Some U; apply HR; exact Hy ✓.
